I usually just go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I've been feeling depressed or sad. Just... my eating habits are weird right now, but my mood feels fine.\n\nDoctor: Did these changes in your eating start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, it did. I had a pretty bad fight with my regional manager at work. That was about a month ago, and this whole thing has been going on ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Since that fight at work, have you been feeling anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, the fight itself was pretty stressful, obviously, but... I wouldn't say I've been walking around feeling anxious or on edge since then. I'm just going about my job like normal. It's really just the eating thing.\n\nDoctor: Are you very worried about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't really care about my weight or how I look. I mean, I'm just an average guy. It's not about trying to lose weight or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have these changes in your eating been causing problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they have. It's... it's definitely affecting things. Like at work, my manager has actually started pulling me off some of my accounts. I guess I've been making mistakes that I normally wouldn't make. And then HR even got involved after I made a call to the employee assistance program. So, I mean, I'm still showing up every day, doing my job, but... it just feels like work, and even things at home, are kind of slipping out of my control right now.\n\nDoctor: Since that fight at work, have you had less appetite?\n\nPatient: Honestly, that's actually the thing that scared me into coming in. It's not just less appetite... my eating has been completely all over the place. Like, some days I'll just forget meals completely, but then other nights I'm tearing through takeout and a bunch of snacks after midnight. It's just really unpredictable right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your eating changed because of the fight with your manager?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I'm not really sure, to be honest. The fight definitely made things more stressful at work, but... I haven't really thought about it causing the eating stuff. It just feels like everything kind of got messed up around the same time, not necessarily that the argument is what changed how I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do these changes in your eating upset you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. It's frustrating, you know? I just want to feel normal again and not have to think so much about whether I'm eating too much or not eating at all. It's just one more thing that feels out of my control right now, and that definitely upsets me.\n\nDoctor: Since the fight with your manager, have you noticed any other changes in your behavior besides your eating?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, not really anything else that stands out. Just the eating thing, mostly. I haven't really noticed anything else being different since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight since this started?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure if I've lost weight, I haven't really been checking the scale. And then other nights, I'm up way past midnight just tearing through takeout and snacks. It's just really unpredictable right now.\n\nDoctor: Have your clothes started fitting looser since this began?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed my clothes fitting any differently, to be honest. Like I said just a minute ago, it's just that my eating habits are completely chaotic right now. That's what really pushed me to come see you. I'll go a whole day skipping meals without realizing it, and then I'm up at 2 AM bingeing on junk food and takeout. It's just totally out of whack.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat even when other people tell you that you look normal?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel fat or anything like that.
